**V. 32. ***{Let them exalt him also in the Congregation of the people}* God ought to be praised and magnified in his Church, not only for special mercies proper to some people and Nations; but also for his general goodness and mercy towards all mankind, wherein no small portion of his glory, among men, does consist.

**V. 33. ***{He turneth rivers into a wilderness}* Jer. 9.12, 13. *Who is the wise man, that may understand this,* &c. and 12.4. *How long shall the land mourn, and the herbs of every field wither, for the wickedness of them that dwell therein?*

*{watersprings}* Heb. *goings out of the waters*.

**V. 34. ***{barrenness}* Heb. *saltness*.

**V. 35. ***{He turneth the wilderness into a standing water}* Isa. 41.18. Psal. 114.8.

**V. 36. ***{that they may prepare}* Or, *so that they prepare*.

**V. 37. ***{which may yield}* Or, *which yield*.

*{which may yield fruits of increase}* Continual increase, and yearly.

**V. 38. ***{multiplied greatly}* Exod. 1.7.

*{and suffereth not their cattle to decrease}* But makes them to increase greatly.

**V. 39. ***{Again they are minished}* As God by his providence does exalt men, so does he also humble them by afflictions, to make them know themselves.

**V. 40. ***{He poureth contempt upon Princes}* Job 12.21. For their wickedness and tyranny, he causes their people and subjects to contemn them.

*{wilderness}* Or, *void place*.

**V. 41. ***{Yet setteth he the poor on high from affliction}* 1 Sam. 2.8. Psal. 113.7, 8.

*{from}* Or, *after*.

*{and maketh him families like a flock}* By giving him many children, which divide themselves into several families.

**V. 42. ***{The righteous shall see it, and rejoice}* Job 22.19. Good men shall rejoice to see God's judgments on the wicked, Psal. 58.10. See upon Prov. 24.17. *Rejoice not,* &c.

*{all iniquity shall stop her mouth}* Job 5.16. That is, all wicked men.

**V. 43. ***{Whoso is wise, and will observe these things}* Jer. 9.12, 13. *Who is the wise man, that may understand this, and who is he, to whom the mouth of the Lord hath spoken, that he may declare it, for what the land perisheth,* &c. See before upon Psal. 25.14. *The secret of the Lord,* &c. and Psal. 28.5. *Because they regard not,* &c. And Hos. 14.9. *Whoso is wise, and he shall understand,* &c.

*{and will observe}* Or, *to observe*.

# PSALM CVIII

## TITLE

*{A song or psalm}* See Notes on the title of Psal. 48.

**Verse 1. ***{O God}* The five first verses of this Psalm are the same with the end of the fifty-seventh Psalm; and the eight last are the same with the end of the 60th Psalm. See the Notes there.

*{my heart is fixed}* This earnest affection declares, that he is both sincere and serious in God's service.

**V. 4. ***{clouds}* Or, *skies*.

**V. 6. ***{That thy beloved may be delivered}* Psal. 60.5.

# PSALM CIX

**Verse 1. ***{Hold not thy peace}* Psal. 83.1.

*{O God of my praise}* Who are the subject of my praise, and gives me cause daily to praise you for the benefits and deliverances.

**V. 2. ***{mouth of the deceitful}* Heb. *mouth of deceit*.

*{are opened}* Heb. *have opened themselves*.

**V. 3. ***{fought against me without a cause}* Psal. 35.7.

**V. 4. ***{but I give myself unto prayer}* To declare that I had none other refuge but you.

**V. 5. ***{rewarded me evil for good}* Psal. 35.12.

**V. 6. ***{Set thou a wicked man over him}* He means either Doeg, or some other familiar friend that did betray him. As concerning those imprecations that follow, see upon Psal. 35.4. *Let them be confounded and put to shame.*

*{Satan}* Or, *adversary*.

**V. 7. ***{be condemned}* Heb. *go out guilty, or wicked*.

*{let his prayer become sin}* Let no suit of his prevail for him, but against him.

**V. 8. ***{let another take his office}* Acts 1.20.

*{office}* Or, *charge*.

**V. 9. ***{fatherless}* Exod. 22.24.

**V. 10. ***{let them seek their bread also out of their desolate places}* Out of their houses destroyed.

**V. 11. ***{Let the extortioner catch all that he hath}* Let the extortioner, with cunning tricks, wipe him of all that he has gotten with much labor.

*{catch all that he hath}* Job 18.9.

**V. 13. ***{Let his posterity be cut off}* Job 18.19.

**V. 14. ***{Let the iniquity of his fathers be remembered with the Lord}* God visits the sins of the parents upon their wicked children, Exod. 20.5.

**V. 16. ***{Because that he remembered not to shew mercy}* He shows the cause why God does plague him after a strange sort, that he was himself cruel towards others.

**V. 17. ***{As he loved cursing, so let it come unto him}* Thus God punishes in kind: those that curse others, shall be cursed of him.

**V. 18. ***{into his bowels}* Heb. *within him*.

**V. 20. ***{From the Lord}* For being destitute of man's help, he fully trusted in the Lord, hoping that he would deliver him.

**V. 21. ***{for thy Name's sake}* Psal. 106.8. As you are named merciful, gracious, and long-suffering, so show thyself in effect.

**V. 23. ***{When it declineth}* See Notes on Psal. 102.11.

*{tossed}* Meaning, that he has no stay nor assurance in this world.

**V. 24. ***{My flesh faileth of fatness}* There is some elegance in the different use of the Preposition מן, which in the first member is positively and properly: and, privatively, or negatively, if we may so speak, causally first, *through:* in the second, *for want of.* Now this leanness that David complains of here, came not for want of need; though he might be in want sometimes in his flights, but not to make him lean: nor from willful fasting; that also he did use sometimes, as elsewhere he professes; but through perpetual cares, and vexations; according to that of his son, *A merry heart doth good like a medicine; but a broken spirit drieth the bones,* Prov. 17.22.

**V. 25. ***{shaked their heads}* Psal. 22.7.

**V. 26. ***{O save me according to thy mercy}* The more he was contemned of men, the more earnest he was in prayer to God.

**V. 27. ***{that this is thy hand}* That it is your work to deliver me, and confound them.

**V. 28. ***{Let them curse, but bless thou}* Let them gain nothing by cursing me.

**V. 30. ***{with my mouth}* Not only in confessing his goodness to myself, but also in declaring it before all the Congregation.

**V. 31. ***{from those that condemn his soul}* Heb. *from the Judges of his soul*. Hereby he shows, that he had not to do with them that were of little power, but with Judges and Princes of the world.

# PSALM CX

## TITLE

*{of David}* Heb. לדוד, which may be translated either *of,* as those Psalms that have Asaph his name prefixed; or *to,* as in many Psalms before, whereof there is no question, but they are David's. Neither is it here only in these Titles, but very frequent upon other occasions in the Scripture, to prefix this particle, or article, of itself more proper to the Dative, to the Genitive: as for example, Jer. 47.6. *O thou sword of the Lord,* Heb. חרב ליהוה, and so elsewhere, frequently.

Here it is the more considerable, because of the consequence of the Psalm to us Christians, and the opposition of the Jews, who would not have it to be David's, but somebody else's, what it affords against them, and for us, the better to refute. Our Savior, Matt. 22.43. quotes it as David's Psalm, which is authority enough for us to believe it is so: but against the Jews, it may further be observed, that it was the common opinion in those days among the Jews, that it was David's, as may be gathered by Christ's words, who speaks it as a thing known and granted, and by the silent approbation of his adversaries, the Scribes and Pharisees, and other Jews.

**Verse 1. ***{The Lord said unto my Lord}* That David himself, prophesying concerning the future Messiah, as he was inspired by the Holy Ghost, speaks this, we have Christ's authority, Matt. 22.44. and the consent of ancienter Jews and Rabbins. But later Rabbins have bethought themselves how to evade it. Some therefore would have this spoken to Abraham, after his conquest of the kings, Gen. 14, by his servant Eliezer. Others, to Hezekiah King of Judah, I know not upon what ground. But the greater part, make these words to be the people's words unto David, congratulating him his settling of the Ark upon Mount Zion, and his own, after his many victories, establishment in the throne.

But if this were granted unto them, yet have they got but little, except they can tell us, how David, or any other King of the Jews, could be styled a Priest, yea an eternal Priest, *after the order of Melchisedec,* as follows here in the 4th verse. To say that the word כהן may be translated Prince, as well as Priest, though we deny not that it is so sometimes, yet it would be here apparently impertinent, and ridiculous, since we read nothing of Melchisedec, extraordinary, as a King: who was but a very petty King in comparison of David, King of Salem, a town of no great account, in those days, but of his Priesthood, we do, as being a Priest of the most High God; and that as Priest, he blessed Abraham, in whom others were blessed; and that Abraham gave him tithes of all, Gen. 14. and Heb. 7.

**V. 2. ***{The rod of thy strength}* Or, *Sceptrum potentiae tuae; The scepter of thy power,* or, *Thy powerful scepter.* A spiritual scepter, yet powerful, notwithstanding the opposition of the great ones of the world, by fire and sword, and other violent means; and of the wise ones of the world, Philosophers, and others; all these notwithstanding, powerful to the conversion of the world unto the Christian faith.

Of this power of Christ's scepter, the Apostle speaks magnificently, 2 Cor. 10.4, 5. *For the weapons of our warfare are not carnal, but mighty through God,* &c. And Eph. 6.12. *For we wrestle not against flesh and blood, but against principalities, against powers,* &c.

*{out of Zion}* *For out of Zion shall go forth the Law, and the word of the Lord from Jerusalem,* Isa. 2.3. And thence it shall spread throughout all the world: and this power chiefly stands in the preaching of the Word. Zion also was mentioned, Psal. 2.6. *I set my King upon my holy hill of Zion;* which Psalm, by the Jewish Expositors, is generally acknowledged to be written concerning Messiah. And if that; there is as much reason for this, though they grant it not.

*{Rule thou, in the midst of thine enemies}* See upon Psal. 2, both Psalms being of one subject, upon verse 2. *The kings of the earth:* and verse 3. *Let us break:* and verse 7. *Thou art my son.*

**V. 3. ***{Thy people shall be willing}* By your Word your people shall be assembled unto your Church, whose increase shall be so abundant and wonderful, as the drops of the dew falling from the womb of the morning.

*{in the beauty of holiness}* An ordinary periphrasis of the Church or Sanctuary. See before, Psal. 29.2. *Worship the Lord in the beauty of holiness:* and Psal. 96.9. *in the beauty of holiness,* or, *in the glorious Sanctuary,* as it is there in the margin. So 2 Chron. 20.21. of Jehoshaphat, *He appointed singers unto the Lord, that should praise the beauty of holiness.* It might as well be translated, supplying the *in,* from other like places, *in the beauty of holiness.*

The comparing of these, and other like places, has made them, though men of great judgment and learning, that have the words translated, *pulcherrime praeparatus:* that is, *gallantly furnished:* or, *completely armed.* See upon next words.

*{from the womb of the morning: thou hast the dew of thy youth}* These words are very variously both translated and expounded. The chief ground of the difference is, partly from the accent in the word שחר, morning, which usually distinguishes between the members of one sentence, or verse; but sometimes is observed to stand for some other use, and not for distinction: and partly from the mistake of divers ancients, taking the מ in משחר for a preposition; and lastly from the ambiguity of the word ילדות, by some translated *juvenius,* or, *juventa:* by others, *nativitas,* or, *generatio.*

Here in our English, as also, by Junius, the accent is observed, as a distinction: But by most others, and those very learned and accurate, the words, *from the womb of the morning,* are joined with those that follow; to this effect; *ex utero aurorae* or, as some others, but not so well, *ab utero, ab aurora, tibi ros generationis,* or, *juventutis tuae:* that is, *from the womb of the morning is the dew of thy youth,* or, *nativity.*

Now for the sense of the words, most ancients have thought that the eternal generation of Christ, the Son of God; and some, both ancient and later Expositors, that his human, or miraculous, Incarnation in the womb of the blessed Virgin, was intended; and there be words indeed, some, that will fit well enough to either. Nevertheless the most and most approved of later Expositors, upon a more accurate search into the words, have judged, that not Christ himself was here spoken of; but the multitude of people that should be converted unto Christ, upon the first preaching of the Gospel: very fitly compared to the morning dew: or more poetically, to the dew of the womb of the morning: both to express the multitude, who should, as it were, over-spread the earth, as the dew does use to cover it; and the manner of their heavenly generation, by the immediate operation of God's Holy Spirit.

Some think that Micah did allude to these words of the Psalmist, where he says; *And the remnant of Jacob shall be in the midst of many people: as dew, as the showers upon the grass that tarrieth not for man, nor waiteth for the sons of men,* Mic. 5.7.

As for the word ילדות, if according to some, it be translated *adolescentia, juventus,* or, *pueritia:* then the reason of it according to some learned Expositors, will be, because all that come to Christ, must, in harmless simplicity, become like little children. Or if that seem too subtle, as I doubt it may, then, *juventus,* or, *pueritia,* may according to others, merely be for *soboles,* that is, a new brood; which in effect comes to the same as, *generatio,* or, *nativitas,* as the word is expounded by them that follow that interpretation.

Other interpretations there be, which I think not needful here to relate. Learned Grotius translates the whole verse thus; *Populus tuus promptissimus erit in die valde pulcherrime praeparatus: Ros (id est, unde proles tibi nascetur) erit ut ros ab utero aurorae.* It agrees, as to this latter part, with that which has already been set down, and commended as the most accurate.

*{from the womb of the morning: thou hast}* Or, *more than the womb of the morning*: *you shall have*, &c. Or, *more than the morning*.

**V. 4. ***{Thou art a priest for ever, after the order of Melchizedek}* Heb. 5.6. and 7.17. See before upon the title. What is here translated, *according to the order,* is in the Original Hebrew, על דברתי, by some, which is most literal, translated, *according to my word.* And because the Apostle, Heb. 5.6, 10. and elsewhere expresses it, in Greek, κατὰ τὴν τάξιν Μελχισέδεκ, some think he had it otherwise in his Copy, or the Septuagint at least, whom he follows. But there is no need of that; since it is certain that such a *Jod*, does sometimes abound at the end: They call it *Jod paragogicum.*

Now of Melchisedec, who he was, and of all things belonging to him, much has been written by divers learned men, both Expositors of the Scriptures, and others. But as yet *sub judice lis est:* neither is it a thing, I think, to be decided by the wit of man. Not here I am sure to be handled; and therefore I forbear.

**V. 6. ***{he shall fill the places with the dead bodies; he shall wound the heads over many countries}* This is objected to us by the Jews, as an argument, that our Christ cannot be the scope and subject of this Psalm, because, say they, he was no Warrior. We answer, that these be figurative and poetical expressions, whereby no more is intended, than that he shall have a glorious conquest, and that no power of the earth shall be able to withstand him.

There is not anything among men, ordinarily, that is entertained with so great an opinion of honor and glory, and lifts up the fantasy more powerfully, than the fame of warlike achievements, great fights, conquests and victories, and spoils, and the like. Such has been the estimation of those things, that all terms of honor, almost, came from thence: and the very name of virtue, ἀρετή, among the Grecians, derives its pedigree from Mars: and κακία, now used for naughtiness, or vice in general, properly signifies cowardliness.

The Scripture therefore speaking to men, and condescending to vulgar apprehensions, when it would set out the glory of anything, it borrows terms and expressions from that which is most glorious among men: *Gird thy sword upon thy thigh, O most Mighty,* &c. and, *Thou art more glorious than the mountains of prey,* &c. and, *Therefore will I divide him a portion with the great, and he shall divide the spoil with the strong,* &c. The Scripture is full of such expressions. God himself, is often set out as a Warrior. The objection is very impertinent. See also upon Psal. 78.65. *Like a mighty man,* &c.

*{many}* Or, *great*.

**V. 7. ***{he shall drink of the brook in the way}* He shall, in the eager pursuit of his enemies, drink hastily of the water next at hand; and being thereby refreshed, pursue his enemies to full victory. Some understand it of Christ's bitter cup in his passion, and glorious resurrection.

*{therefore shall he lift up the head}* He shall triumph after his victory: or, he shall lift up the head of Christ.

# PSALM CXI

This is one of the Alphabetical Psalms, of which see upon Psal. 25. *Unto thee, O Lord,* &c. at the end of the Psalm.

**Verse 1. ***{Praise ye the Lord}* Heb. *Hallelujah*.

*{I will praise the Lord}* See Psal. 25.1. The Prophet declares that he will praise God both privately and openly, and that from the heart, as he that consecrates himself wholly and only unto God.

**V. 2. ***{great}* He shows that God's works are a sufficient cause wherefore we should praise him, but chiefly, his benefits towards his Church.

*{sought out of all them that have pleasure therein}* The more sweetness God's children find in his works, the further they dive into them.

**V. 3. ***{honourable and glorious}* Heb. *honor and glory*.

**V. 4. ***{He hath made his wonderful works to be remembered}* Heb. *He has made a remembrance of his wonderful works*.

*{gracious and full of compassion}* Psal. 86.5. and 103.8.

**V. 5. ***{He hath given meat unto them that fear him}* God has given to his people all that was necessary for them, and will do still. Thus the word is taken, Prov. 30.8, 31.15. Mal. 3.10. Some understand it of feeding the people without labor, in the wilderness, as God gives prey to the beasts, Matt. 6.27. Psal. 104.21, 147.9.

*{meat}* Heb. *prey*.

*{he will ever be mindful of his covenant}* He looks not at their sins, but at his own promise.

**V. 7. ***{The works of his hands are verity}* As God promised to take care of his Church; so, in effect, does he declare himself just and true in the government of the same.

*{all his commandments are sure}* It is likely he means his promises, which are commands to his creatures to provide for his people. See verse 9. Psal. 105.8.

**V. 8. ***{stand fast}* Heb. *are established*.

**V. 10. ***{The fear of the Lord is the beginning of wisdom}* Job 28. Prov. 1.7. and 9.10. The Hebrew word is ראשית, which some translate, as it may well, *caput, summa,* not *the beginning,* but *summa,* or, *perfection,* and other like expressions in Ecclesiasticus. Both senses may stand. For there be degrees as of wisdom, so of the fear of God. There is no degree of that fear so low, but is a step to wisdom; nor any true perfection of wisdom, attainable by man, but is reducible to the fear of God. Both degrees are included, Deut. 4.6. *Keep therefore and do them,* statutes and judgments, the verse before, *for this is your wisdom, and your understanding,* &c.

*{a good understanding}* Or, *good success*.

*{that do his commandments}* Heb. *that do them*.

*{his praise endureth for ever}* The praise of God, or, of him that keeps God's commandments.

# PSALM CXII

**Verse 1. ***{Praise ye the Lord}* Heb. *Hallelujah*.

*{Blessed is the man that feareth the Lord}* See Psal. 25.1. He means, with that reverent fear which is in the children of God, which causes them to delight wonderfully in the Word of God. See note upon Psal. 32.1. *Blessed is he whose transgression,* &c.

*{feareth the Lord}* Psal. 128.1.

**V. 2. ***{His seed shall be mighty upon earth, &c.}* Of this and other temporal blessings here annexed to godliness, see upon Psal. 37. *Fret not thy self,* &c.

**V. 3. ***{Wealth and riches shall be in his house}* The godly shall have abundance and contentment, because their heart is satisfied in God only.

*{his righteousness endureth for ever}* That is, the fruit of his righteousness in heaven: or, continue to posterity.

**V. 4. ***{Unto the upright there ariseth light in the darkness}* Psal. 97.11. Righteous men shall have comfort in adversity, for they comfort others, verse 5. Psal. 97.11.

**V. 5. ***{sheweth favour, and lendeth}* Psal. 37.26. Or, *is showing favor, and lending*; to wit, frequently.

*{and lendeth}* He shows what is the fruit of mercy, To lend freely, and not to gain, and so to measure his doings, that he may be able to help where need requires, and not to bestow all on himself, Psal. 37.26. Luke 6.34, 35.

*{discretion}* Heb. *judgment*.

**V. 6. ***{in everlasting remembrance}* He shall be praised for his good deeds long after his death, Prov. 10.7.

**V. 7. ***{He shall not be afraid of evil tidings}* Prov. 1.33.

**V. 9. ***{He hath dispersed}* 2 Cor. 9.9. The godly pinch not niggardly, but distribute liberally, as the necessity of the poor requires, and as his power is able: yet he gets by it; for the reward of his bounty abides for ever.

*{righteousness endureth for ever}* verse 3.

*{his horn shall be exalted for ever}* His power, glory, and prosperous estate, Psal. 75.10.

**V. 10. ***{The wicked shall see it, and be grieved, he shall gnash with his teeth}* So it falls out sometimes, even in this world: but, of that other, and far more considerable world, to which, in a more general and absolute sense, all these promises and threatenings, as upon Psal. 31 is showed, must be referred, our Savior directly; *There shall be weeping, and gnashing of teeth, when ye shall see Abraham, and Isaac, and Jacob, and all the Prophets in the Kingdom of God, and you yourselves thrust out,* Luke 13.28.

*{gnash with his teeth}* Psal. 37.12.

*{and melt away}* Psal. 58.8. The blessings of God upon his children, shall cause the wicked to die for envy.

*{the desire of the wicked shall perish}* Prov. 10.28.

# PSALM CXIII

**Verse 1. ***{Praise ye the Lord}* Heb. *Hallelujah*.

*{praise the name of the Lord}* By this often repetition he stirs up our cold dullness to praise God, seeing his works are so wonderful.

**V. 2. ***{Blessed be the Name of the Lord}* Dan. 2.20.

**V. 3. ***{From the rising of the sun unto the going down of the same}* Mal. 1.11. Isa. 59.19.

**V. 4. ***{The Lord is high above all nations}* If God's glory shine throughout all the world, and therefore he ought to be praised of all men, what great condemnation were it to his people, among whom chiefly it shines, if they should not earnestly extol his Name.

*{his glory above the heavens}* Psal. 8.1.

**V. 5. ***{Who is like unto the Lord our God}* Psal. 89.6.

*{dwelleth}* Heb. *exalts himself to dwell*.

**V. 6. ***{Who humbleth himself to behold the things that are in heaven}* God, out of his high seat of glory, vouchsafes to provide for all creatures heavenly and earthly, Psal. 138.6.

**V. 7. ***{He raiseth up the poor out of the dust}* 1 Sam. 2.8. Psal. 107.41.

*{and lifteth up the needy out of the dunghill}* He prefers the poor to higher honor, and giving the barren children, shows that God works in his Church, not only by ordinary means, but also by extraordinary.

**V. 9. ***{to keep house}* Heb. *to dwell in an house*.

# PSALM CXIV

**Verse 1. ***{When Israel went out of Egypt}* Exod. 13.4.

*{people of strange language}* Psal. 81.5.

**V. 2. ***{Judah was his sanctuary}* A people sanctified to himself, Exod. 19.5, 6.

*{dominion}* Heb. *dominions*.

**V. 3. ***{The Sea saw it, and fled}* Exod. 14.21.

*{Jordan was driven back}* Josh. 3.13.

**V. 4. ***{The mountains skipped like rams}* Seeing that these dead creatures felt God's power, and after a sort saw it, much more his people ought to consider it, and glorify him for the same.

*{lambs}* Heb. *sons of the sheep*.

**V. 7. ***{Tremble thou earth at the presence of the Lord}* Some question is made about the original word חולי, whether an Infinitive, and if so, whether the *Jod* be vacant or no; or whether an Imperative, as here rendered. But since the word itself as we find it here, has all the properties of a regular Imperative feminine, as it should, joined with *earth:* we may surely enough, especially with the consent of most Interpreters, take it for an Imperative.

But though it be of an Imperative sense in the original, yet whether to be translated by an Imperative, is another question. Some very exact in all punctilios and idioms of the original Text, observe well, that the Imperative, in the Hebrew, is sometimes put for the preterit: as particularly Psal. 22.9. *He trusted,* or *rolled himself upon,* *on the Lord:* it is in the Hebrew, directly, *roll thou:* or, *trust thou:* as by Psal. 37.5. where the same word, and form is, apparent, in the Imperative, which being impertinent, Expositors, for want of this observation, whereof there be more examples in Scripture, have been troubled what to make of it.

We may therefore as well, if we will, translate here, *The earth trembled,* or, *Tremble thou earth:* and it may fit the coherence somewhat better, perchance; the whole Psalm relating to things passed, *When Israel went out of Egypt,* &c. verse 1, &c.

**V. 8. ***{Which turned the rock into a standing water}* Num. 20.11. Psal. 107.35.

# PSALM CXV

**Verse 1. ***{Not unto us, O Lord}* Because God promised to deliver them, not for their sakes, but for his Name, Isa. 48.11. Ezek. 36.32. therefore they ground their prayer upon his promise.

*{but unto thy name give glory}* Honor thyself, by delivering us from our enemies.

**V. 2. ***{Where is now their God?}* Psal. 42.10. and 79.10. The wicked seeing that God accomplishes not his promise, imagine, they scoff at his people, saying, The men were wont to boast of God's help, but now he has failed them, Deut. 32.37, 38.

**V. 3. ***{But our God is in the heavens}* Psal. 135.6. Though they be deceived, for our God is in heaven.

*{whatsoever he pleased}* No impediments can let the working of the omnipotent to serve his will.

**V. 4. ***{Their idols are silver, &c.}* Psal. 135.15. Seeing neither the matter nor the form can commend the best of them; it follows, that there is nothing why they should be esteemed.

**V. 7. ***{They have hands, but they handle not}* He shows what great vanity it is to ask help of them, which not only cannot help in themselves, but also lack sense and reason.

**V. 8. ***{are like unto them}* As much without sense as the stone.

**V. 9. ***{He is their help and their shield}* The help of those that trust in him; as Psal. 84.11, 12. *For the Lord God is a Sun and shield: blessed is the man that trusteth in him.* Or, by a permutation of persons, ordinary in the Hebrew tongue, *our help, and our shield,* Psal. 33.20.

**V. 10. ***{O house of Aaron}* For they were appointed by God instructors and teachers of faith and religion for others, Mal. 2.7.

**V. 11. ***{Ye that fear the Lord, trust in the Lord}* Interpreters give a reason, why after the *house of Israel,* whereby the generality of the people is intended, and *the house of Aaron,* all Priests and Levites, divided from the rest, not only by reason of their office; but also, because they only had no inheritance of land among the Israelites; and subsisted by a peculiar right, mentioned, he should add, *ye that fear the Lord,* as a peculiar and distinct order; and cause, say they, among both Priests and people there were many hypocrites; who though outwardly so, yet really, or inwardly, as the Apostle speaks, were not either priests or Israelites.

The interpretation, though by many approved of, seems not so proper in this place: the Psalmist directing his speech by way of exhortation to all that went under that name, among whom, no doubt, were hypocrites: that is, that lived not according to their profession, might be as fit an object of his exhortation, as any others. Nay, it might more probably be objected, that the exhortation was not very needful, if none were intended, but those who already, truly and cordially fear God; which may very well, though to include some trust in God.

I think therefore the interpretation of the ancients, many times laid aside, where later, that are not comparable to them are taken in their room, to be preferred, who take these words, *Ye that fear the Lord,* not in a common use and notion; but as a title, or denomination among the Jews, appropriated to a peculiar sort of people, who among the Jews, being no Jews born, nor yet circumcised, absolute proselytes: but yet part of the Jews, in general, because they lived among them, and worshipped the same God with them, and were obliged to many performances, though not such as the natural Jews, or absolute proselytes were.

We meet with them here again, verse 13. *He will bless them,* &c. and Psal. 118. Such also, under the same title of φοβούμενοι τὸν Θεόν, are mentioned, Acts 10.2. and 13.16.

**V. 12. ***{he will bless us}* That is, he will continue his good will toward his people, be they of what degree or condition soever, Psal. 128.1.

**V. 13. ***{and}* Heb. *with*.

**V. 15. ***{which made heaven and earth}* And therefore can govern and continue all things therein, Jer. 10.11.

**V. 16. ***{the heavens are the Lord's}* To be the seat of himself, Isa. 66.1.

*{the Lord's}* Or, *for the Lord*.

*{to the children of men}* To use; but he has kept the right of all to himself.

**V. 17. ***{dead praise not the Lord}* Psal. 6.5. and 30.9. and Isa. 38.18. See the Note upon Psal. 6.5.

*{neither any that go down into silence}* Though the dead praise God's glory in heaven, yet they praise him not here in the Congregation, Psal. 6.5.

**V. 18. ***{But we will bless the Lord}* Dan. 2.20. When we are delivered and preserved alive.

# PSALM CXVI

**Verse 1. ***{I Love the Lord}* He grants, that no pleasure is so great, as to feel God's help in our necessity, and that nothing stirs up our love to him more.

*{I love the Lord because he}* Heb. *I love because the Lord*.

*{I love the Lord because he hath heard}* Or, *I am satisfied, since that the Lord has heard*.

**V. 2. ***{as long as I live}* Heb. *in my days*. So oft as I shall be in distress.

**V. 3. ***{The sorrows of death compassed me}* Psal. 18.5, 6.

*{got hold upon me}* Heb. *found me*.

**V. 5. ***{and righteous}* So just, that he will deliver them that are unjustly oppressed.

**V. 6. ***{I was brought low, and he helped me}* I was destitute of human help and counsel.

**V. 7. ***{Return unto thy rest, O my soul}* Psal. 42.11. and 46.5. Return to the composed estate, and be no more disquieted, now God has freed you.

**V. 9. ***{I will walk before the Lord}* Or, *I shall walk*; that is, the Lord will deliver me, I shall not die in this danger.

*{land}* Heb. *lands*.

**V. 10. ***{I believed, therefore have I spoken}* 2 Cor. 4.13. Though my affliction was great, yet my faith made me pray to God for deliverance.

**V. 11. ***{my haste}* Psal. 31.22.

*{All men are liars}* Rom. 3.4. Samuel and all, who promised me the Kingdom, that am like to die before I can attain to it, 1 Sam. 27.1. Some make this to be the sense; *I said in my* Heb. חפזי *trembling,* or, *consternation*, *All men are liars;* that is, There is no trust to man, or to human help: but to God there is, which must be supplied, as in speech oftentimes, the opposite member is, who can help, when all other help does fail. I will not therefore despair.

**V. 13. ***{I will take the cup of salvation}* Such a cup as the Israelites used to take when they came to offer sacrifice, and to give thanks for their deliverance; to which custom some refer that which we read of our Savior, Luke 22.17. *And he took the cup,* &c. as also the sacramental cup, which afterwards was instituted by our Savior, in lieu of that former rite, Luke 22.20, and is by the Apostle called *the cup of blessing,* 1 Cor. 10.16. and the whole Sacrament was afterwards called Εὐχαριστία, that is, A thanksgiving, or, *An action of thanks*.

*{salvation}* Heb. *salvations*.

**V. 15. ***{Precious in the sight of the Lord}* See Notes on Psal. 72.14. and upon Psal. 49.11. *by this honor.*

**V. 16. ***{the son of thy handmaid}* Psal. 86.16.

**V. 18. ***{I will pay my vows unto the Lord}* Besides the sacrifice of thanksgiving enjoined, Lev. 7.12. I will sacrifice those things which I vowed, in my adversity, to offer, when I should be delivered.

# PSALM CXVII

**Verse 1. ***{O Praise the Lord, all ye nations, praise him, all ye people}* Rom. 15.11. We should not make any question of the mystical sense, or application to Christ, and the Gentiles, though we had not St. Paul's authority for it, Rom. 15.11. it being so natural, and obvious, at least, to any that are read in the Prophets, where so often foretell this conversion of the Gentiles unto Christ, by the preaching of the Gospel, and speak of it so gloriously, as an argument of joy unto all Nations.

However, though Expositors content themselves with this, yet it has already been said upon divers other Psalms, I know no reason why we should pass by, or if not obvious, neglect the inquisition of a more literal and immediate sense. But indeed, obvious enough here, as I conceive. For though God did in great mercy adopt to himself the Jews, for a season, as a peculiar people; and style himself their God in a more peculiar manner: yet did he never so forsake other nations, or withdraw his kindness from them, as that they should not have occasions both ordinary, and extraordinary, to glorify him for his goodness and mercies.

Besides sundry passages in other Psalms: we have one entire Psalm, one of the most sublime for the style of it, in the whole book, written to that purpose: the subject whereof is often repeated, *O that men would praise the Lord for his goodness, and for his wonderful works to the children of men,* Psal. 107. verses 8, 15, 21, 31. See there upon the argument of the Psalm, and upon verse 6. *Then they cried,* &c. and, *and he delivered,* &c.

If therefore then, even when true religion was the privilege of one nation, and God in some manner appropriated unto it: yet even then holy Prophets, inspired by the Holy Ghost, did call upon all men, and all nations to praise God; not rhetorically neither, or hyperbolically, as when dumb creatures are called upon to praise God: as is well observed by Calvin, though upon another occasion, but simply and literally: to praise God, I say, to acknowledge *his goodness and his wonderful works,* as Psal. 107. or as it is here, *his merciful kindness, and his truth,* ver. 2. how great and miserable is their blindness, and sottish infatuation; who in these days, ever since Christ, of public grace and mercies, we speak not here of saving grace and mercy, not outwardly and publicly discernable, wherein all nations have equal interest, would engross God wholly to themselves, and as they conceive his mercy and goodness to be their peculiar; so neither would they have God to accept of the prayers or praises of any others, but such as come from themselves? All the heresies of the world put together, cannot amount to more impiety: and yet is the sottishness and ridiculousness of the opinion, greater, than the impiety.

**V. 2. ***{and the truth of the Lord}* Interpreters in their mystical Expositions, as they distinguish between *people,* by which they understand the Jews particularly, and *nations;* that is, the Gentiles, in the former verse: so here, *merciful kindness,* they would have to belong to the *nations;* because, say they, God was not obliged to them by any promise: and *truth,* to the Jews.

But even they that do not distinguish between *people* and *nations,* in the former, for all do not, I confess, yet here between *mercy,* or merciful kindness, and *truth* they do. We have spoken of the difference elsewhere, upon Psal. 57.10. *For thy mercy is great:* and there also showed how they might be taken for one and the same thing. Which sense, I think, would do well here too: although we may say, if need be, that God had bound himself to the Gentiles by his Prophets, as well as to the Jews, for a future Redeemer: and so his *truth,* even so distinctly taken, might belong unto them too, according to the mystical sense: neither do we want promises of another nature, made by God to mankind in general; for performance whereof, they might be called upon to praise him, to make good that other sense, which we have spoken of: although, as I said before, there is no need of that distinction here.

*{people}* Heb. *peoples*.

# PSALM CXVIII

**Verse 1. ***{O Give thanks unto the Lord, &c.}* 1 Chron. 16.8. Psal. 106.1. and 107.1. and 136.1. Because God, by creating David King, showed his mercy towards his afflicted Church, the Prophet does not only himself thank God, but exhorts all the people to do the same.

**V. 3. ***{Let the house of Aaron now say}* See Notes on Psal. 115. verse 10.

**V. 5. ***{in distress}* Heb. *out of distress*. The more troubles oppress us, the more instant ought we to be in prayer.

*{in distress the Lord answered me}* Psal. 120.1.

*{in a large place}* Psal. 18.20.

**V. 6. ***{The Lord is on my side, I will not fear}* Psal. 56.4, 11. Heb. 13.6.

*{on my side}* Heb. *for me*.

*{what can man do unto me?}* He doubted not but God would maintain him against all enemies, because he had placed him in the kingdom, Psal. 27.1, 3.

**V. 7. ***{with them that help me}* Psal. 54.4.

**V. 8. ***{than to put confidence in man}* He shows, that he had trusted in vain, if he had put his confidence in man, to have been preferred to the kingdom: but, putting his trust in God, he obtained it.

**V. 9. ***{It is better to trust in the Lord, than to put confidence in princes}* Psal. 146.2.

**V. 10. ***{All nations compassed me about}* That is, all round about: as Philistines, Amalekites, Ammonites, Moabites, &c. whom David at several times had to do with.

*{will I}* Or, *I did*, as Psal. 119.23.

*{destroy them}* Heb. *cut them off*. Or, *I did destroy them*.

**V. 11. ***{will}* Or, *did*.

**V. 12. ***{bees}* Deut. 1.44.

*{they are quenched as the fire of thorns}* See upon Psal. 58.9. *Before your pots can feel the thorns.* The Hebrew word דעכו here, as by most others translated, *they are quenched,* is by some thought to have a contrary signification also: and both by the Greek Interpreters, and by the Chaldee Paraphrast, is expressed, *they did burn.* But either of the two may serve here: the sense of the words will be the same.

*{destroy}* Heb. *cut down*.

**V. 13. ***{Thou hast thrust sore at me}* He notes Saul, his chief enemy.

**V. 14. ***{The Lord is my strength and song}* Exod. 15.2. Isa. 12.2.

*{and song}* In that he was delivered, it came not of himself, nor of the power of man, but only of God's favor, therefore he will praise him cheerfully, Psal. 69.30.

**V. 15. ***{The voice of rejoicing}* He means, that other good men should have cause of joy and thankfulness, as well as he, because, by raising him God had restored the Church to quietness.

**V. 16. ***{The right hand of the Lord is exalted}* So that they that are far and near may see his mighty power.

**V. 17. ***{I shall not die}* My enemies' plots could not kill me.

**V. 18. ***{The Lord hath chastened me sore}* God corrects with moderation, to amendment, not to destruction, Jer. 10.24. and chap. 30. verse 11.

**V. 19. ***{Open to me the gates of righteousness}* Psal. 24.7, 9.

*{the gates of righteousness}* He wills that the doors of the Temple should be opened by the Priests or Levites, whose office it was, that he might enter to praise God.

**V. 20. ***{into which the righteous shall enter}* So Isa. 26.2. The wicked cannot enter, 2 Chron. 23.19. A type of forbidding access to God's house.

**V. 22. ***{The stone which the builders refused}* Though Saul's counselors, and Israelites, great men, refused to accept of me for their King, yet God has given me the kingdom. So the Priests, Scribes and Pharisees rejected Christ, whom God had anointed.

*{The stone which the builders refused, is become the head stone of the corner}* Matt. 21.42. Mark 12.10. Luke 20.17. Acts 4.11. 1 Pet. 2.4.

**V. 23. ***{This is the Lord's doing}* Heb. *This is from the Lord*.

**V. 24. ***{This is the day which the Lord hath made}* Wherein God showed his mercy, by making me King, and delivering me. So the day of publishing Christ's Kingdom was a choice day. The words may also have a more particular application to the day of Christ's resurrection, by which, as the Apostle teaches, especially, he was *declared to be the Son of God with power, according to the Spirit of holiness,* Rom. 1.4. See Psal. 2.7. *Thou art my Son:* and, *this day.*

*{we will rejoice}* Or, *let us rejoice*.

**V. 25. ***{save now; I beseech thee}* The joyful acclamations which the people used, when David came to the Tabernacle to give thanks for the Kingdom received. They prayed God to save their King and them: so they do for Christ, Matt. 21.9, 15. Mark 11.9. Luke 19.38.

**V. 26. ***{we have blessed you out of the house of the Lord}* We the Priests use to bless the people publicly, Num. 6.23. Deut. 10.8.

**V. 27. ***{God is the Lord, which}* Or, *The Lord is that God*, which.

*{which hath shewed us light}* That is, prosperity under the Kingdom of David, and Christ, Psal. 97.10. So on the other side, darkness for affliction, often in Scripture.

*{sacrifice}* Heb. *The festival*; that is, the beast, killed in the feast.

*{the horns of the altar}* Till you come to the horns of the altar, Lev. 4.7. That is, bring it thither to be killed. Others more literally interpret it of the binding of the beast, that was to be offered, to the horns of the altar. It is very likely, that those horns particularly mentioned, Exod. 27.2. were intended for that use; though it be not there expressed.

# PSALM CXIX

This is one of the Alphabetical Psalms, of which, see upon Psal. 25. at the end. The chief end of this Psalm, as by the matter, words and style does appear, seems to have been to disengage the hearts and affections of men that have a sense of piety, from worldly objects, and to settle them upon the study and meditation of piety and godliness: to which end, the frequent reading of it, where there is a disposition to goodness, does marvelously conduce; as also to keep the soul in a constant plight of humble, meek, and harmless simplicity: the certain, and necessary consequent of true mortification, and sanctification.

It is penned with great plainness and simplicity of style, suitable to the matter, and fitted for all capacities. The same things are often inculcated; and the same words repeated. There be some words, ten in number, all which almost signify one thing, so often repeated, that in the whole Psalm, as has been observed by some, consisting of 176 verses in all, there is but one verse, where some of them, one, or more, are not used.

The words are these: תורה, חוקים, מצוה, פקודים, עדות, משפטים, אמרה, דבר, צדק, דרך: law, statutes, commandment, precepts, testimonies, judgments, saying, word, righteousness, way.

The number will not hold in the English, because two of the Hebrew words, דבר and אמרה, are expressed by one in English, to wit, *word.* However, we may make up the number, adding *ordinances,* as the word משפטים, properly *judgments,* is sometimes translated; as verse 91.

Of these ten Hebrew words, and of their frequent repetition throughout the whole Psalm, but one verse, the 122, excepted, we find in two, Bucerus and Vatablus; and they had it from some of the Rabbins. For both Kimchi, and Ben Ezra, affirm the such thing: though they speak but of eight words; and make a further mystery of it, because the whole Psalm is penned, or digested by Octonaries. But it is never good to press things upon trust, where a man may use his own eyes. For first, there may be more verses found besides the 122, where none of those either eight or ten words are used. As for example verse 90. I will not have any unadvisedly to ascribe this, as a probable oversight, to any variation of the Original Copies, but mere oversight of their eyes.

Now if any acquainted with these frequent repetitions, I would have them consider, that they were but according to the first intention of the author, the better to express his zeal to the Word of God, and therefore fall not within compass of tautologies: and that there is as much are in repeating the same words, so often, with so much variation of sense; as in finding diversity both of matter and words.

**Verse 1. ***{Blessed are the undefiled in the way, who walk in the law of the Lord}* See Psal. 128.1.

*{the undefiled}* Here they are not called blessed, which think themselves wise in their own judgment, nor which imagine to themselves a certain kind of holiness, but they whose conversation is without hypocrisy, Ezek. 18.15.

*{undefiled}* Or, *perfect, or, sincere*.

*{in the way}* A good life is a good way, that leads to heaven.

**V. 2. ***{that keep his testimonies}* God's law testifies of his will, which else were unknown to us.

**V. 3. ***{They also do no iniquity}* They live not in a sinful course of life: Sin is not their trade, as it is wicked men's, 1 John 3.9.

*{do no}* Or, *that do, or commit*.

*{walk}* Or, *that walk*.

**V. 5. ***{O that my ways were directed}* David acknowledges his imperfection, desiring God to reform it, that his life may be conformable to God's Word.

**V. 6. ***{Then shall I not be ashamed, when I have respect unto all thy commandments}* Shame, the fruit and effect of sin: temporal, or in this world: *what fruit had ye then in those things, whereof ye are now ashamed,* Rom. 6.21. Eternal; *And many of them that sleep in the dust of the earth, shall awake; some to everlasting life, and some to shame, and everlasting contempt,* Dan. 12.2.

**V. 7. ***{thy righteous judgments}* Heb. *judgments of your righteousness*. That is, your precepts; which contain perfect righteousness.

**V. 8. ***{O forsake me not utterly}* He refuses not to be tried by temptations; but fears, lest he faint, if God succor not his infirmity in time, but leave him wholly to himself.

**V. 9. ***{Wherewith shall a young man cleanse his way}* Because youth is most given to licentiousness, he chiefly warns them to frame their lives betimes to God's Word, Eccles. 11.9. and 12.1.

**V. 11. ***{Thy word have I hid in mine heart}* If God's Word be hid in our hearts, we shall be more able to resist the assaults of Satan; which, therefore he desired carefully to treasure up, Psal. 37.31. Luke 2.19, 51. See also upon Psal. 40.10. *I have not hid thy righteousness within my heart.*

**V. 14. ***{I have rejoiced in the way of thy testimonies}* The Prophet does not boast of his virtues, but sets forth an example for others to follow God's Word, and leave worldly vanities.

*{in}* Or, *for*.

**V. 17. ***{that I may live}* He shows, that we ought not to desire to live, but to serve God: and that we cannot serve him aright, except he take away our natural ignorance by the light of his Spirit, that we may understand the spiritual meaning of his Word.

*{and keep}* Or, *to keep*.

**V. 18. ***{Open}* Heb. *Reveal*.

**V. 19. ***{I am a stranger in the earth, hide not thy commandments from me}* Gen. 47.9. 1 Chron. 29.15. Psal. 39.12. Heb. 11.13.

*{I am a stranger in the earth}* Seeing man's life in this world, is but a passage to heaven, what should become of him, who knows not the way by nature, if God's Word did not guide him thither?

**V. 20. ***{My soul breaketh for the longing that it hath unto thy judgments at all times}* Psal. 42.1. and 63.1. and 84.1.

**V. 21. ***{Thou hast rebuked the proud that are cursed}* In all ages you have plagued all such, which maliciously and contemptuously depart from your truth. There is some difference among Interpreters about the ordering of the words. Those that observe the accent, agree with our English here. Those that do not, in number as many, if not more, order them thus: *Thou hast rebuked,* or, *destroyed,* *the proud: cursed are they, which do erre from thy commandments.* This I take to be the more plausible construction: and the accent, we know, is often not regarded. However, the difference is of no great moment.

**V. 22. ***{Remove from me}* Or, *Roll from upon me*.

**V. 23. ***{Princes also did sit and speak against me}* verse 46. When the powers of the world gave false sentence against me, your Word was a guide and a counselor, to teach me what to do, and to comfort me.

**V. 24. ***{Thy testimonies also are my delight}* Heb. *delights*. That is, they did greatly delight me.

*{my counsellors}* Heb. *men of my counsel*.

**V. 25. ***{My soul cleaveth unto the dust}* That is, I am like one overcome in battle, mortally wounded, and tumbling in the dust, or one in danger to die, and turn to dust, Psal. 44.25. and 22.15. and without your Word I cannot live.

**V. 26. ***{I have declared my ways}* I have laid open my business, and dangers to you in great difficulties, as to my chief friend, and you have advised me ever for my good.

*{teach me thy statutes}* Psal. 25.4. and 27.11. and 86.11.

**V. 28. ***{My soul melteth for heaviness}* Psal. 107.26.

*{melteth}* Heb. *drops*.

*{according unto thy word}* That is, your promise. If God did not maintain us by his Word, our life would drop away like water, Psal. 22.14.

**V. 29. ***{Remove from me the way of lying}* Keep me out of those false ways, that corrupt nature teaches, and let your law guide me in the true way to happiness. To this *way of lying,* that is, false doctrine, and erroneous opinions concerning God and his worship, is presently opposed, verse 30, *the way of truth:* of which see there.

**V. 30. ***{I have chosen the way of truth}* *The way of truth,* as 2 Pet. 2.2. *And many shall follow their pernicious ways, by reason of whom the way of truth shall be evil spoken of:* That is, true religion. To this דרך אמונה, or, *way of truth,* is contrary דרך שקר in the foregoing verse; which might have been, as it is afterwards verse 104, more properly translated *the false way:* that is, erroneous, heretical opinions, *the way of lying:* as if he intended falsehood in matter of speech, or actions; which certainly is not the meaning. Neither is there any contrariety, as some may fancy, in David's words, when he prays to God to remove from him, or to preserve him from false opinions; and presently protests, that he has chosen *the way of truth.* For first, in case that he were already in the right way, as is likely, nay, certain; yet, so was once his son Solomon, who nevertheless became afterwards a great Idolater. And the Apostle says, *Let him that thinketh he standeth, take heed lest he fall,* 1 Cor. 10.12.

But secondly, when he says, *he hath chosen the way of truth,* he says no more, but that it is his earnest desire, and firm resolution to adhere to the truth, so soon as God shall reveal it unto him. But then there is an outward, and an inward revelation; both necessary; David had both in a great measure; yet still prays for both; neither is any so perfect in this kind, but may say with him in the Gospel, *Lord I believe; help thou mine unbelief.*

**V. 32. ***{when thou shalt enlarge my heart}* By this he shows, that we can neither choose good, cleave to God's Word, nor run forward in his ways, except he make our hearts large to receive his grace, and willing to obey, by freeing from straits, filling us with joy and comfort, Esay 60.5. Psal. 4.1.

**V. 33. ***{Teach me, O Lord, the way of thy statutes}* He shows, that he cannot follow on to the end, except God teach him oftentimes, and lead him forward.

**V. 34. ***{with my whole heart}* Not only in outward conversation, but also with inward affection.

**V. 36. ***{And not to covetousness}* He prays specially against this evil affection, as being the root of all evil, 1 Tim. 6.10. and withdrawing men from the love of God and goodness, Matt. 6.24. 1 John 2.15.

**V. 37. ***{Turn away}* Heb. *Make to pass*.

*{Turn away mine eyes}* Because they are the chiefest inlets of sinful objects, Job 31.1. Isa. 33.15. 2 Sam. 11.2.

*{from beholding vanity}* Worldly things, that have no substance in them, nor are but shadows, Prov. 23.5.

**V. 39. ***{for thy judgments are good}* Let me not suffer reproach for obeying your Word, which is good. Or, Let not your Word and your Truth, which I fear more than anything else, that can happen to me, suffer reproach in my sufferings, as though I had boasted falsely; or you couldst not perform. So also verse 22. and ver. 41, 42, 43.

**V. 40. ***{quicken me in thy righteousness}* Restore life and strength to me, according to your faithful promise, verse 37.

**V. 41. ***{Let thy mercies come also unto me}* He shows, that God's mercy and love, is the first cause of our deliverances.

**V. 42. ***{So shall I have wherewith to answer him that reproacheth me}* Or, *So shall I answer him that reproacheth me in a thing, or in ought.* When your promise is effected, I can stop the mouth of my scoffing adversaries, Psal. 107.42.

**V. 43. ***{take not the word of truth utterly out of my mouth}* Make me not ashamed to speak of your promises any more, by not performing those you have made to me already.

**V. 45. ***{And I will walk at liberty}* I shall be free from all distress of soul and body, verse 32.

*{at liberty}* Heb. *at large*.

**V. 46. ***{before kings}* He shows, that the children of God ought not to be daunted from giving testimony to God's truth, by any power or fear of Princes, Dan. 3.13, 16. Matt. 10.18, 19. verse 23.

**V. 48. ***{My hands also will I lift up unto thy commandments}* I will address myself unto the doing of your will, as a workman does to his work.

**V. 49. ***{Remember the word unto thy servant}* Though he feel God's hand still to lie upon him, yet he rests on his promise, and comforts himself therein.

**V. 51. ***{The proud have had me greatly in derision}* Meaning, the wicked, which contemn God's Word, and tread his religion under foot.

**V. 52. ***{I remembered thy judgments of old}* That is, the examples, whereby you declares thyself to be Judge of the world.

**V. 53. ***{Horror hath taken hold upon me}* A vehement zeal for your glory, and indignation against the wicked: or, dread of persecution from them.

*{because of the}* Heb. *from the*.

**V. 54. ***{in the house of my pilgrimage}* In all the places where I have been banished: or, in the course of my life, which is but a sorrowful pilgrimage, Gen. 47.9.

*{pilgrimage}* Heb. *pilgrimages*.

**V. 55. ***{in the night}* Even when others sleep, Psal. 63.6.

**V. 56. ***{This I had}* This comfort in afflictions from you, day and night, verse 54, 55.

**V. 57. ***{Thou art my portion, O Lord}* Psal. 16.5. and 142.5. It may be read, *O Lord, I have said, my portion is to keep thy words:* that is, I am persuaded that to keep your law is an heritage, and great gain for me, John 4.34.

*{my portion, O Lord}* Or, *O Lord, my portion*, verse 126.

**V. 58. ***{favour}* Heb. *face*.

**V. 59. ***{I thought on my ways}* He called himself to account for his former courses, and amended what he found amiss in them, Lam. 3.40.

**V. 61. ***{bands}* Or, *company*, 1 Sam. 10.10. No calamity, nor persecution, has hitherto so far wrought upon me, as to make me forsake your truth, or to go against my conscience, in anything. So also verse 87.

**V. 62. ***{because of thy righteous judgments}* God's Word is a great benefit, and he is worthy of great praise for giving it to us: or, he promises to praise God night and day for his judgments executed upon his enemies, according to his Word.

*{thy righteous judgments}* Heb. *your judgments of righteousness*.

**V. 63. ***{I am a companion of all them that fear thee}* Not only by mutual consent, but also by aid and succor, Psal. 16.3. to any godly, be they never so mean.

**V. 64. ***{The earth, O Lord, is full of thy mercy}* As you gives every creature good things according to the nature of it, so give my soul spiritual knowledge.

**V. 65. ***{Thou hast dealt well with thy servant}* Having proved by experience, that God was true in his promise he desires that he would increase in him knowledge and judgment.

**V. 67. ***{Before I was afflicted, I went astray}* Jer. 31.18. The use of God's rods, is to bring us home to God, verse 71. Heb. 12.10, 11.

**V. 70. ***{Their heart is as fat as grease}* Their heart is indurate and hardened, puffed up with prosperity, and vain estimation of themselves, Psal. 17.10. Isa. 6.10.

**V. 71. ***{It is good for me that I have been afflicted}* He confesses that before he was chastened, he was rebellious, as man by nature is, verse 67. See before upon Psal. 34.19. *Many are the afflictions of the righteous,* &c.

**V. 72. ***{The law of thy mouth is better unto me than thousands of gold and silver}* Psal. 19.10. Prov. 8.11.

**V. 73. ***{Thy hands have made, and fashioned me}* Job 10.8.

*{Thy hands have made me}* Because I am your creature, teach me to serve you, who are my Maker, Psal. 95.6. and 100.2, 3. and 138.8.

**V. 74. ***{They that fear thee, will be glad when they see me}* When God shows his grace toward any, he testifies to others, That he fails not them that trust in him, and so makes them glad also.

**V. 75. ***{right}* Heb. *righteousness*.

*{that thou in faithfulness hast afflicted me}* That you have showed your faithfulness, even in correcting me, your correction making for my good, Heb. 12.10. 1 Pet. 4.19.

*{thou in faithfulness}* Or, *it is faithfulness that you*.

**V. 76. ***{for my comfort}* Heb. *to comfort me*.

**V. 77. ***{that I may live}* That I may be freed from these afflictions, for men in adversity are like dead men.

*{thy law is my delight}* See Notes on verse 24.

*{delight}* Heb. *delights*, as verse 92.

**V. 79. ***{turn unto me}* Be comforted by my example, and join with me, verse 74.

*{those that have known thy testimonies}* He shows that the true fear of God is not without the knowledge of his Word.

**V. 80. ***{that I be not ashamed}* Which I should be, if I did not keep your statutes, verse 6, 31.

**V. 81. ***{My soul fainteth for thy salvation}* Though my strength fail me, yet my soul groans and sighs, resting still in your Word.

**V. 82. ***{Mine eyes fail for thy word}* Psal. 69.3.

**V. 83. ***{like a bottle in the smoke}* Like as a skin bottle or bladder that is shriveled in the smoke.

**V. 84. ***{How many are the days of thy servant?}* How long will you afflict your servant? Psal. 89.47.

**V. 85. ***{The proud have digged pits for me, which are not after thy law}* This is spoken by a figure, whereof there be many examples in best Writers, called λιτότης, or, extenuation; which is, when we speak of a thing, which of itself is horrid, and which would be thought so, in terms of extenuation: as if we say of a man, or any particular fact, that it is not very commendable, when we intend, that it is most abominable. So is the word *illaudatus* in Virgil.

He might also perchance, as there be many secret allusions in the Scripture, allude to the Law concerning digging of pits, Exod. 21.33, 34. aggravating their wickedness from thence, that the Law of God having provided for the security of beasts, by taking order for the covering of necessary pits and wells, their wickedness must needs be most execrable, that digged secret pits of purpose for men to fall into them.

Or he might allude to שיחה *meditatio:* which differs in nothing from שוחה that signifies a pit, but in a little prick over one of the letters: as if he would say, While your Law was my שיחה, *sicha,* or meditation, verse 97 and 99, they busied themselves in digging שיחות, *sichoth,* or pits for me. Examples of such secret allusions we have had divers already. See upon Psal. 106. verse 15. and 29.

The Greek and Latin translate the word *fabulationes,* which may be thought somewhat to this purpose: and questionless *sicha,* or, *sichoth* is the word that they had in their Copies; or at least in their thoughts, when they translated *fabulationes,* or ἀδολεσχίας: but as to the sense of the whole member, they had another aim, as appears by the whole context of their translation: which is; *Narraverunt mihi iniqui fabulationes: sed non ut Lex tua. Διηγήσαντό μοι παράνομοι ἀδολεσχίας,* &c.

*{The proud have digged pits for me}* They have not only oppressed me violently, but also craftily conspired against me, Psal. 35.7.

**V. 86. ***{faithful}* Heb. *faithfulness*.

*{help thou me}* He desires the true God to help him against false-dealing persecutors.

**V. 87. ***{upon earth}* The uttermost that they could do, could not make me forsake your law.

**V. 88. ***{Quicken me after thy lovingkindness}* Suffer me to live: or, raise me out of these troubles. See Notes on verse 40, 77.

**V. 89. ***{For ever, O Lord, thy word is settled in heaven}* Because none should esteem God's Word, according to the changes of things in this world, he shows, that it abides in heaven, and so abides immutable: or, the permanency of it, is seen in the continuance of the heavens: so of the earth, verse 90. and so verse 91. *They continue,* that is, heaven and earth, *for they are thy servants.*

**V. 90. ***{unto all generations}* Heb. *to generation and generation*.

*{abideth}* Heb. *stands*.

**V. 91. ***{They continue this day}* Seeing the earth and all creatures remain in that estate, wherein you have created them, much more your truth remains constant and unchangeable.

**V. 92. ***{my delights}* See Notes on verse 24.

**V. 93. ***{with them thou hast quickened me}* See Notes on verse 77, 88.

**V. 94. ***{I am thine, save me}* He proves by the effects, that he is God's child, because he seeks to understand his will, that he may do it.

**V. 96. ***{I have seen an end of all perfection}* There is nothing so perfect on earth, but it has an end: only God's Word lasts forever, Isa. 40.8. So Matt. 24.35. *Heaven and earth shall pass away; but my words shall not pass away.*

**V. 97. ***{it is my meditation all the day}* Our daily meditating of God's law, is a sure token that we love it, Psal. 1.2.

**V. 98. ***{thou through thy commandments hast made me}* Or, *Each of your commandments has made me*.

*{they are ever with me}* Heb. *it is ever with me*.

**V. 99. ***{I have more understanding than all my teachers}* Whosoever does submit himself only to God's Word, shall not only be safe against the practices of his enemies, but also learn more wisdom, than they that profess it, and are men of experience, 2 Tim. 3. See also upon Psal. 107.43. *Whoso is wise,* &c. and 111.10. *The fear of the Lord is the beginning of wisdom,* &c.

**V. 100. ***{I understand more than the ancients}* Job 32.8.

**V. 102. ***{for thou hast taught me}* God does acquaint us with his will, that we may do it, Psal. 86.11.

**V. 103. ***{How sweet are thy words unto my taste}* Psal. 19.10.

*{taste}* Heb. *palate*.

**V. 104. ***{therefore I hate every false way}* verse 128.

**V. 105. ***{Thy word is a lamp unto my feet}* Of ourselves we are but darkness, and cannot see, except we be enlightened with God's Word.

*{lamp}* Or, *candle*, Prov. 6.23.

**V. 106. ***{I have sworn}* Neh. 10.29. By solemn oath and promise, he stirred up himself to embrace God's Word.

*{and I will perform}* It does appear by many former verses, how little David, or whoever was the author, did ascribe to his own strength; so that this, *I will perform,* must be understood of his present settled resolution, and confidence of God's assistance for the future. But neither then must this performance be understood of perfect legal obedience; not liable to any lapses, or infirmities: but of such as God in mercy does accept, where there is a real will and endeavor. See Rom. chap. 7. and upon Psal. 18.20. *The Lord rewarded me according to my righteousness.*

**V. 107. ***{quicken me}* See Notes on verse 77, 88.

**V. 108. ***{the freewill offerings of my mouth}* Give me cause by delivering me, to praise you, and offer what I have freely vowed, Psal. 116.17, 18. and 54.6.

**V. 109. ***{in my hand}* I am continually in danger of my life. A thing in the hand is easily taken away. See the like phrase, Judg. 12.3. 1 Sam. 19.5. and 28.21. Job 13.14.

**V. 110. ***{have laid a snare for me}* Psal. 140.5.

**V. 111. ***{as an heritage for ever}* Deut. 33.4. I esteem no worldly thing so, but made your Word mine inheritance.

**V. 112. ***{to perform}* Heb. *to do*.

**V. 113. ***{I hate vain thoughts}* Whosoever will have God's Words aright, must abhor all fantasies and imaginations of himself and others, in God's service, and the way to heaven.

**V. 114. ***{my hiding place}* Psal. 32.7.

*{and my shield}* Psal. 18.2.

**V. 115. ***{Depart from me, ye evildoers}* Matt. 7.23. Psal. 6.8. And hinder me not in keeping God's law.

**V. 116. ***{let me not be ashamed of my hope}* Which I shall be, if I have not the thing hoped for, Rom. 5.5.

**V. 117. ***{Hold thou me up}* He desires God's continual assistance, lest he should faint in this race, which he had begun.

**V. 118. ***{their deceit is in falsehood}* The crafty practices of them who contemn your law, shall be brought to nought; as they are caught in their own craft: *Atque sic eum frustrantur,* or, *solidi existimant:* as one of the ancient Comics expresses it.

**V. 119. ***{puttest away}* Heb. *causes to cease*.

*{like dross}* Ezek. 22.18, 22. which infect the metal.

*{dross}* Heb. *drosses*. That is, the vilest dross.

**V. 120. ***{My flesh trembleth for fear of thee}* Your judgments do not only teach me obedience, but also cause me to tremble, considering mine own weakness, Hab. 3.16.

**V. 122. ***{Be surety for thy servant for good}* Put thyself between mine enemies and me, as if you wert my pledge.

**V. 123. ***{Mine eyes fail for thy salvation}* verse 82.

*{the word of thy righteousness}* Or, *your righteous word*.

**V. 124. ***{Deal with thy servant according unto thy mercy}* He boasts not of his service to God, but declares that he is bound to him by his grace, so he would continue his favor.

**V. 126. ***{It is time for thee}* The Prophet shows, that when the wicked have brought all things to confusion, and God's Word to utter contempt, then it is God's time to help and send remedy.

*{to work}* To perform your promise to me, lest I be destroyed by my foes, who will be kept in by no laws any longer, no not by the law of God.

**V. 127. ***{Therefore I love thy commandments above gold, yea, above fine gold}* Psal. 19.10. Prov. 8.11.

*{Therefore I love thy commandments}* Seeing they discourage men, so that few dare serve you, I do it the more diligently; lest your service fall to the ground.

**V. 128. ***{I hate every false way}* verse 118, 104. Whosoever dissents from the purity of your Word.

**V. 129. ***{Thy testimonies are wonderful}* Containing secret mysteries, so that I am moved with admiration and reverence of them.

**V. 130. ***{The entrance of thy words giveth light}* The simple idiots, that submit themselves to God, have their eyes opened, and their minds illuminated, so soon as they begin to read God's Word, Psal. 19.7, 8.

**V. 131. ***{and panted}* My zeal toward your Word. A metaphor from those that run after a thing till they be out of breath: or, from thirsty creatures.

**V. 132. ***{Look thou upon me, &c.}* Psal. 106.4.

*{as thou usest to do unto those that love thy name}* Or, *according to the custom towards those, &c*.

**V. 133. ***{let not any iniquity have dominion over me}* Psal. 19.13.

**V. 135. ***{to shine upon thy servant}* Psal. 4.6.

**V. 136. ***{Rivers of waters run down mine eyes}* Such ought to be the zeal of God's children, when they see his Word contemned.

**V. 137. ***{upright are thy Judgments}* It becomes those that profess God to be righteous, to acknowledge his judgments, and to live according to them.

**V. 138. ***{righteous}* Heb. *righteousness*.

*{faithful}* Heb. *faithfulness*.

**V. 139. ***{My zeal hath consumed me}* Psal. 69.9.

*{consumed me}* Heb. *cut me off*.

**V. 140. ***{Thy word is very pure}* Gold has not so much purity, as your Word is perfection itself, Psal. 12.6.

*{pure}* Heb. *tried, or refined*.

**V. 141. ***{I am small and despised}* This David, an ordinary man, might say of himself, in his beginning, or when in persecution, and distress; as Psal. 22.6. *a worm and no man: a reproach of men, and despised of the people.* But the author of the Psalm, according to most Interpreters, being uncertain, we cannot certainly tell in what respect, or upon what occasion, he uses these words.

*{yet do I not forget thy precepts}* This is the true trial, to stick to God's law in adversity.

**V. 143. ***{taken hold on me}* Heb. *found me*.

**V. 144. ***{and I shall live}* So that the life of man, without the knowledge of God, is death.

**V. 145. ***{I cried with my whole heart}* He shows that his affections and whole heart were bent to Godward in his dangers.

**V. 146. ***{and I shall keep}* Or, *that I may keep*.

**V. 148. ***{Mine eyes prevent the night watches}* He was more earnest in the study of God's Word, than they that keep the watch were in their charge, Psal. 130.6.

**V. 149. ***{judgment}* Or, *custom*, verse 132.

**V. 150. ***{that follow after mischief}* That earnestly seek to undo me.

**V. 152. ***{Concerning thy Testimonies I have known of old, that thou hast founded them for ever}* Among men, says Aristotle, τὰ δίκαια κινούμενα, that is just at one time, which at another is unjust; and on the contrary: which makes many men think, that nothing in nature is just or right, but by opinion only. But among the gods, says he, perchance it is not so. It is a great comfort to godly men, in times of general confusion, when wickedness is established by Law, and all things turned upside down to know, that there is a just God in heaven, who will in due time judge and reward, not according unto the opinions of the times, but according to the reality of things, revealed unto men in his holy Word.

*{Concerning}* Or, *Out of*.

**V. 154. ***{Plead my cause, and deliver me}* Psal. 35.1. and 43.1.

*{according to thy word}* For without God's promise, there is no hope of deliverance.

**V. 156. ***{Great}* Or, *Many*.

*{judgments}* Or, *customs*.

**V. 158. ***{and was grieved}* See Notes on verse 136.

**V. 159. ***{consider how I love thy precepts}* Our love of God's law, is a good argument to move God to deliver us.

**V. 160. ***{Thy word is true from the beginning}* Heb. *The beginning of thy word is true.* Since you first promised, even to the end, all your sayings are true.

**V. 161. ***{Princes have persecuted me without a cause}* The threatenings and persecutions of Princes could not cause me to shrink from confessing you, whom I more fear than men, verse 23, 42. or, they sought to kill me, but I durst not hurt them when I could, because I feared your law, as 1 Sam. 24.6.

**V. 164. ***{seven times a day do I praise thee}* That is, often and sundry times, Prov. 24.16. and 26.25.

**V. 165. ***{Great peace have they that love thy law}* God takes care for their good, that take care to do him service according to his law.

*{nothing shall offend them}* Heb. *they shall have no stumbling block*. This must be understood of such offending, or stumbling, as ends in a total and final defection, or apostasy. For he says before, *Trouble and anguish,* &c. verse 143. and *I beheld the transgressors, and was grieved,* &c. verse 158. And St. Paul of himself, *Who is weak, and I am not weak? who is offended, and I burn not?* 2 Cor. 11.29.

**V. 166. ***{Lord, I have hoped for thy salvation}* Gen. 49.18.

*{and do thy commandments}* They that make no conscience of obeying God, cannot hope for deliverance from him.

**V. 168. ***{for all my ways are before thee}* Job 34.21. I had no respect of men, so as to offend you, but set you always before mine eyes, as the judge of my doings, and therefore studied to please you.

**V. 169. ***{Let my cry come near before thee}* My earnest prayer.

*{according to thy word}* As you have promised to be a teacher unto all them that depend upon you.

**V. 170. ***{Let my supplication come before thee}* My prayer for favor.

**V. 171. ***{My lips shall utter praise}* verse 7. This word signifies, to pour forth continually, like a fountain, Psal. 78.2.

*{when thou hast taught me thy statutes}* All his prayer and desire is to profit in the Word of God.

**V. 173. ***{for I have chosen thy precepts}* Josh. 24.22. Prov. 1.29. To follow it of mine own accord: or, among all other things, for my chief good and treasure, wherewith I am satisfied.

**V. 174. ***{I have longed for thy salvation, O Lord}* verse 166.

*{delight}* Heb. *delights*.

**V. 175. ***{let thy judgments help me}* That is, your provident care over me, and wherein you will judge mine enemies.

**V. 176. ***{I have gone astray like a lost sheep}* Isa. 53.6. *All we like sheep,* &c. You therefore that are the good Shepherd, Luke 15.4. seek me, and bring me home into the Church, which is your fold.

Of the manifold errors and deviations of men in point of life; and pursuit of happiness, much has been written by ancient Philosophers, whereby it appears that they were not unsensible, or ignorant of the miserable condition of man, naturally, without a guide: though they knew not the cause of it, Adam's fall, and much less, the remedy, by Christ. *Errare, atque viam palantes quaerere vitae,* is the business of all, or most men in the world, even of many that think very well of their present condition, according to one of their Poets. See also upon Psal. 23.3. *in paths of righteousness.*

# PSALM CXX

## TITLE

*{A song of degrees}* Of lifting up: for they did lift up their voice sometimes in singing, 2 Chron. 20.19. or, *of excellencies*, 1 Chron. 17.17. or, to be sung on the steps of the Temple; which some of the Rabbins say were fifteen in number; between the *atrium Israelis,* or the men's Court, and the women's according to the number of Psalms here following, that bear that inscription, on high days: or, at their several stations, as they returned from Babylon.

**Verse 1. ***{In my distress I cried unto the Lord}* Psal. 118.5. Albeit, the children of God ought to rejoice, when they are in extremity; yet it is a great grief to the flesh, to hear evil for well doing.

**V. 3. ***{What shall be given unto thee? or, what shall be done unto thee, thou false tongue?}* Or, *What shall the deceitful tongue give unto thee? or, what shall it profit thee? He assures himself, that God would turn their craft to their own destruction*.

*{done}* Heb. *added*.

**V. 4. ***{Sharp arrows of the mighty, with coals of Juniper}* Or, *It is as the sharp arrows of the mighty man with coals of Juniper*, Psal. 64.3.

*{Sharp arrows of the mighty}* God will send on them piercing sorrows, Psal. 45.5. Deut. 32.23. Ezek. 5.16. Psal. 140.10.

*{coals of juniper}* Heb. *of junipers*. That is, of many junipers, which will last.

**V. 5. ***{in Mesech}* These were people of Arabia, which came from Japhet, Gen. 10.2.

*{of Kedar}* Of Ishmael's posterity, Gen. 25.13. which used to live in tents, and remove from place to place, for the pasture of the cattle, Cant. 1.5.

*{That I sojourn in Mesech, that I dwell}* Not those very people, among whom he never was, but people like them for cruelty. This is the translation of all, or most later Interpreters: and of the Chaldee Paraphrast also. But ancient Greek Interpreters, not the Septuagint only, but others also, with the vulgar Latin, take this *Mesech* here, not for a proper name, but for a noun substantive, properly signifying length, or protraction: and here, as they conceive, importing length of time, or long continuance: Οἴ μοι ὅτι ἡ παροικία μου ἐμακρύνθη: that is, *Woe is me, that my sojourning in a strange land is prolonged.* So the LXX. and other two Greek translations, to the same effect.

A late learned man, who has taken great pains about Scripture Geography, does endeavor to assert this version of the LXX. in this place, as true and genuine, against the Chaldees, and other later ones: and in my judgment, he has done it, if not undoubtedly, yet very plausibly. I will not find others against so many translators, of fame and credit: I myself shall take the liberty, to embrace what I think most probable.

One of his reasons is; *Mesech,* when taken for a proper name in Scripture, as in Moses, and Ezekiel, often, it is always, this one controverted place excepted, joined with *Tubal.* It is in Ezekiel, at least five times: and always so joined. In Esdras also it is found so joined. Now here being joined with Kedar, a second argument, it is more improbable it should be a proper name, because Kedar all grant to be a country of Arabia: whereas Mesech is by most, and among others by Diodati, upon the place, taken for *Moscovia:* a Region very far distant from Arabia. More to this purpose the Reader may find in the said Author, if he please.

*{for peace}* Or, *a man of peace*.

# PSALM CXXI

**Verse 1. ***{I Will lift up mine eyes unto the hills, from whence cometh my help}* I am sorry to find some Expositors, men otherwise of no small account, so ready to find faults, where there is so little reason. We have met with them already once or twice upon that strain: *vox inconsiderata,* and the like. Here again they except against these words, as uttered in a fit of impatience, or infidelity: which though they confess it did not last long, but was presently checked by the Psalmist himself in the next verse: yet I conceive they do him a great deal of wrong, in imposing their own fancies, upon his words, and expressions: which I conceive not only blameless, but very emphatical, in a quite contrary sense.

They spend many words upon this argument, to make their imaginary charge good: but I will not spend many, to relate them. It shall suffice me, to have given the Reader warning, and the Prophet need no other vindication, than to have his words made so clear, which we hope we shall, that there shall be no ground left for any suspicion.

Judea is, was I am sure, and is still, I think, a very mountainous Country. The whole land is called *a hill,* for that reason, Exod. 15.17. according to best Interpreters. Now because men, multitudes of men, upon high mountains, may be seen far off; they that did expect the help of men, or armies, coming to their relief, when their condition did require succor, it is likely they did often lift up their eyes towards the mountains, round about. Certain it is, that upon this situation of the Country, and common use of men, in expectation; many speeches are grounded in the Scriptures; in the Prophets especially.
