**V. 11. ***{Rid me, and deliver me from the hand of strange children}* verses 7, 8.

**V. 12. ***{That our sons may be as plants}* He desires God to continue his benefits toward his people, that there may be a hopeful posterity after them.

*{that our daughters may be as corner stones polished}* Ancient artificers were wont to carve their pillars, in palaces and sumptuous edifices, especially, which did bear the main building, into the form of grave matrons and virgins. Such were those that were called *Caryatides,* whom Vitruvius and others speak of, and describe: though the reason of that particular name, if the common interpretation be true, be much later than David's time. But they had other names too, which might be ancienter. It is very likely that David does here allude to some such custom.

*{polished}* Heb. *cut*.

**V. 13. ***{That our garners may be full}* That we may have corn not only to eat plentifully, but also to lay up abundantly.

*{all manner of store}* Heb. *from kind to kind*.

**V. 14. ***{That our oxen may be strong to labour}* That our cattle may be very serviceable to us.

*{strong to labour}* Heb. *able to bear burdens*; or, *laden with flesh*.

**V. 15. ***{Happy is that people that is in such a case}* Psal. 33.12. and 65.4. If they be blessed that enjoy outward prosperity, much more happy are they that are in God's favor.

# PSALM CXLV

## TITLE

*{praise}* See the Title of Psalm 25.

This is the last of the Alphabetical Psalms; of which, see before upon Psal. 25 at the end.

**Verse 1. ***{I Will extol thee}* Psal. 100.1. He shows what sacrifices are pleasant and acceptable unto God, even praise and thanksgiving: and seeing that God still continues his benefits towards us, we ought never to be weary of praising him for the same.

**V. 3. ***{Great is the Lord}* Hereby he declares, that all power is subject unto God; and that no worldly promotion ought to obscure God's glory.

*{and his greatness is unsearchable}* Heb. *and of his greatness there is no search*.

**V. 4. ***{One generation shall praise thy works to another}* Isa. 38.19. Psal. 73.4.

*{to another}* Forasmuch as the end of man's creation, and of his preservation, in this life, is to praise God, therefore he requires, that not only we ourselves do this, but cause all others to do the same.

**V. 5. ***{works}* Heb. *things, or words*.

**V. 6. ***{of thy terrible acts}* Of your terrible judgments against the wicked.

*{declare thy}* Heb. *declare it*.

**V. 7. ***{They shall abundantly utter}* As water runs out of a Fountain, Jer. 6.7.

*{the memory of thy great goodness}* Your great blessings, which ought to be remembered.

**V. 8. ***{The Lord is gracious, and full of compassion}* Exod. 34.6, 7. Num. 14.18. Psal. 86.5, 15. and 103.8.

*{and full of compassion}* He describes after what sort God shows himself to all his creatures, though our sins have provoked his vengeance against all: he shows himself merciful, not only in pardoning the sins of his children, but also in doing good to wicked men, albeit they feel not the sweet comfort of God's benefits.

*{of great mercy}* Heb. *great in mercy*.

**V. 9. ***{over}* Or, *toward*.

**V. 11. ***{They shall speak of the glory of thy kingdom}* The praise of your glory appears in all your creatures: and though the wicked would obscure the same by their silence, yet the faithful are ever speaking of it.

**V. 12. ***{his mighty acts}* God's servants speak of his great acts, that others may take notice of them.

**V. 13. ***{an everlasting Kingdom}* Heb. *a kingdom of all ages*, 1 Tim. 1.17.

**V. 14. ***{The Lord upholdeth}* All that are uphold from falling, are upheld by God: but some fall, and never rise.

*{all that fall}* Who, being in misery and affliction, would faint and perish, if God did not uphold them, and therefore they ought to reverence him that reigns in heaven, and suffer themselves to be governed by him.

*{raiseth up all those that be bowed down}* Psal. 146.8.

*{all those that be bowed down}* Ready to fall under the burden of their miseries. See upon Psal. 146.8.

**V. 15. ***{The eyes of all wait upon thee}* To wit, as well of man, as of beast.

*{wait upon thee}* Or, *look unto you*.

*{thou givest them their meat in due season}* Psal. 104.27.

**V. 17. ***{The Lord is righteous in all his ways}* He praises God, not only for his beneficence to all his creatures, but also for his justice in all his proceedings.

*{holy}* Or, *merciful, or, bountiful*.

**V. 18. ***{The Lord is nigh unto all them that call upon him}* Not in essence only, but in affection: he is ready to grant their requests, Isa. 55.6.

*{in truth}* In sincerity of heart, as all the faithful do, without hypocrisy.

**V. 19. ***{He will fulfil the desire of them that fear him}* For they ask, or wish for nothing, but according to his Will, 1 John 5.14.

**V. 21. ***{let all flesh bless his holy Name for ever and ever}* Let all men praise him. So Psal. 65.2.

# PSALM CXLVI

**Verse 1. ***{Praise ye the Lord}* Heb. *Hallelujah*.

*{Praise the Lord, O my soul}* Psal. 103.1.

*{O my soul}* He stirs up himself, and all his affections to praise God.

**V. 2. ***{While I live, will I praise the Lord}* Psal. 104.33.

**V. 3. ***{Put not your trust in Princes, nor in the son of man, in whom there is no help}* Psal. 118.8, 9. See also upon Psal. 82.7. *ye shall die like men,* &c.

*{Put not your trust in Princes}* That God may have all praise. Here he forbids all vain confidence, showing that by nature we are more inclined to put our trust in creatures, than in God the Creator.

*{help}* Or, *salvation*; or, because he cannot help himself.

**V. 4. ***{he returneth to his earth}* Of which he was made, Gen. 2.7. and 3.19.

*{his thoughts perish}* As their excellent devices, and vain opinions, whereby they flattered themselves, and imagined wicked enterprises against others.

**V. 5. ***{Happy is he that hath the God of Jacob for his help}* Jer. 17.7.

*{whose hope is in the Lord his God}* Psal. 144.15.

*{in}* Heb. *on*.

**V. 6. ***{Which made heaven and earth, the sea, and all that therein is}* Gen. 1.1.

*{Which made heaven and earth}* He encourages the godly to trust only in the Lord, both, for that his power is able to deliver them from all dangers, and because for his promise sake, his will is most ready to do it.

**V. 7. ***{Which executeth judgment for the oppressed, which giveth food to the hungry}* Psal. 103.6.

*{for the oppressed}* Whose faith and patience for a while he tries, but at length he punishes the adversaries, that he may be known to be the Judge of the world.

*{the Lord looseth the prisoners}* Psal. 68.6. and 107.10.

**V. 8. ***{The Lord openeth the eyes of the blind, the Lord raiseth, &c.}* Though it be most true, literally taken, that God opens the eyes of the blind, &c. yet it is more agreeable to the context, that the words be taken metaphorically, of gracious unexpected deliverances from extreme misery, as long imprisonment, hard bondage, and the like: as *prisoners,* and *captives,* elsewhere. See also upon Psal. 57.6. *my soul,* and 145.14. and 69.23. *Let their eyes be darkened.*

*{the Lord raiseth them that are bowed down}* Psal. 145.14.

*{the Lord loveth the righteous}* Though he visits them by affliction, hunger, imprisonment, and such like trials; yet his fatherly love and pity never fails them, yea, rather to his, these are signs of his love, Heb. 12.6.

**V. 9. ***{The Lord preserveth the strangers}* Meaning, all them that are destitute of all worldly means, and succor, as being in a strange place, where none knows, or regards them.

*{the fatherless and widow}* Deut. 10.18. Psal. 68.5.

*{the way of the wicked he turneth upside down}* Psal. 1.6.

**V. 10. ***{The Lord shall reign for ever}* Exod. 15.18. He assures the Church, that God reigns for ever, for the preservation of the same.

# PSALM CXLVII

**Verse 1. ***{It is good to sing praises unto our God}* Psal. 92.1.

*{for it is pleasant}* He shows wherein we ought to exercise ourselves continually, and to take delight, to wit, in praising God, for that is pleasing to him.

*{and praise is comely}* Psal. 33.1.

**V. 2. ***{The Lord doth build up Jerusalem}* Because the Lord is the founder of the Church, it cannot be destroyed though the members thereof be dispersed, and seem as it were, for a time, to be cast off.

*{he gathereth together the outcasts of Israel}* Deut. 30.3.

**V. 3. ***{He healeth the broken in heart}* With affliction, or with sorrow for sin.

*{wounds}* Heb. *griefs*.

**V. 4. ***{He telleth the number of the stars}* Though it seem to man incredible, that God should assemble his Church, being so dispersed, yet nothing can be too hard for him, that can number, and name all the stars. The stars, according to the Scripture phrase, are accounted as the sand of the Sea, innumerable. Which some think to be spoken only according to common opinion: as many other things are there. Ancient Astronomers indeed are said to have reduced them to a certain number, according to their several magnitudes: they tell us of a thousand and some odd hundreds, in all. But this must be understood of those that could clearly be discerned. For many others have been observed since; and of late, by Galileo to a very great number. It may stand therefore for undoubted truth, that they are to man, innumerable: and so does Aristotle too, positively maintain. Calling of them by their names, imports a perfect knowledge of them; or, as some others, the distinguishing of every one of them to certain places and functions.

*{he calleth them by their names}* Isa. 40.26.

**V. 5. ***{his understanding is infinite}* Heb. *of his understanding there is no number*, Isa. 40.28. Rom. 11.33.

**V. 6. ***{he casteth the wicked down to the ground}* For the more high that the wicked climb, the greater is their fall in the end, Psal. 73.18, 19.

**V. 7. ***{Sing unto the Lord}* Heb. *Answer*: That is, sing by turns, Exod. 15.21. and 32.18. Or, *answer God's goodness by thankfulness and obedience*.

**V. 8. ***{Who covereth the heaven with clouds}* He shows, by the examples of God's mighty power, goodness, and wisdom, that we can never want most just occasion to praise God.

*{who prepareth rain for the earth, who maketh grass to grow upon the mountains}* Psal. 104.13, 14.

**V. 9. ***{He giveth the beast his food, and to the young ravens which cry}* Job 38.41. Psal. 104.27, 28.

*{to the young ravens which cry}* For their crying is, as it were a confession of their need, which cannot be relieved without God: then if God show himself mindful of the contemptible fowls, can he suffer them to die with famine, whom he has assured of life everlasting? Job 38.41. Matt. 6.26.

*{which cry}* Or, *when they cry*.

**V. 10. ***{he taketh no pleasure in the legs of a man}* God is thus bountiful to the creatures, not for any worth in them, but for his love to his people.

**V. 11. ***{in them that fear him, in those that hope in his mercy}* Psal. 33.18.

**V. 12. ***{hope in his mercy}* Heb. *wait for his mercy*.

**V. 13. ***{he hath strengthened the bars of thy gates}* He does not only furnish his Church with all things necessary, but preserves also the same, and makes it strong against outward force.

**V. 14. ***{He maketh peace in thy borders}* Heb. *who makes your border peace*.

*{finest of the wheat}* Heb. *fat of wheat*. Deut. 32.14. Psal. 81.16.

**V. 15. ***{He sendeth forth his commandment}* His secret working in all creatures, is as a commandment to keep them in order, and to give them motion and force.

*{his word runneth very swiftly}* For, immediately, and without resisting, all things obey him. See upon Psal. 107.20. *He sent his word.*

**V. 16. ***{He giveth snow like wool: he scattereth the hoar frost like ashes}* He compares snow to wool, and hoar frost to ashes; not only because of some outward resemblance in color, shape, &c. but also because of the like effects, it being the property of snow, as Naturalists observe, to warm the earth; and of hoar frost, to burn, even to ashes, sometimes; as they say. This effect of the hoar frost some ascribe to the coldness of it; it being the property of intensive cold to burn. But that is denied by others; for then, say they, the snow should burn plants, and buds too, because it is colder than hoar frost. Their resolution therefore of that matter is, that the cause is not known, and to be accounted no less than miraculous. There is an apparent allusion in the original words, between כפור, כאפר, and יפזר.

**V. 19. ***{He sheweth}* As before he called God's secret working in all his creatures, his word, so here he means the will of God revealed in the Scriptures, given to his Church, as a most precious treasure.

*{his word unto Jacob, his statutes and his judgments unto Israel}* Mal. 4.4.

*{his word unto Jacob}* Heb. *his words*: That is, the ten words, or commandments. He gave them the law moral, ceremonial, judicial, Mal. 4.4.

**V. 20. ***{they have not known them}* The cause of this difference is, God's free mercy to his Church, which has therefore great cause to praise him, Rom. 3.1.

# PSALM CXLVIII

**Verse 1. ***{Praise ye the Lord}* Heb. *Hallelujah*.

*{from the heavens}* Rev. 5.13. You heavenly creatures praise God in the heavens.

**V. 2. ***{all his angels}* Psal. 103.20, 21. Because they are members of the same Church, he sets them before our eyes, which are most willing hereunto, and by their prompt obedience, teach us to do our duty.

**V. 3. ***{sun and moon}* In that God's glory shines in these insensible creatures, this their beauty, and orderly motion, is a continual praising of God, Psal. 19.1, 2, &c.

*{stars of light}* Job 38.7.

**V. 4. ***{ye heavens of heavens}* You highest heavens, 1 Kings 8.27. called the third heaven, 2 Cor. 12.2. where the Saints and Angels are. The sky where the stars are, is the second heaven: the air, where the fowls fly, is the first, whereof [uncertain].

*{ye waters}* that is, the rain-waters lodged in the clouds, Gen. 1.7. Job 26.9.

*{above the heavens}* Above the lower region of the air. See also upon Psal. 104.3. *Who layeth the beams of his chambers,* &c. And upon Psal. 24.2. *upon the seas.*

**V. 5. ***{for he commanded, and they were created}* Gen. 1.3, 6. Psal. 33.9.

**V. 6. ***{a decree which shall not pass}* Psal. 119.91. Job 38.33. Jer. 31.35, 36. and 33.25.

**V. 7. ***{ye dragons}* Rather Whales, for they live in the deeps, Gen. 1.21.

**V. 8. ***{stormy wind fulfilling his word}* Which come not by chance, or fortune, but by God's appointment, Psal. 147.15, 16.

**V. 9. ***{Mountains and all hills}* Great and little hills, fruitful and unfruitful trees, wild beasts and tame, worms and birds.

**V. 10. ***{flying fowls}* Heb. *bird of wing*.

**V. 11. ***{Kings of the earth}* For the greater gifts that any has received, and the more high one is preferred, the more bound is he to praise God for the same: but neither high nor low, condition or degree, can be exempted from this duty.

**V. 13. ***{for his name alone is excellent}* His name only is excellent of itself: creatures have no more excellency than he communicates to them.

*{excellent}* Heb. *exalted*.

*{his glory is above the earth and heaven}* And therefore is worthy to be praised by Kings and Angels.

**V. 14. ***{He also exalteth the horn of his people}* The dignity, power, and glory of his Church, Psal. 75.10.

*{the praise of all his saints}* Or, *he is the praise of all his saints*, Luke 2.32. Or, *which is*.

*{even of the children of Israel}* By reason of his covenant made with Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob.

# PSALM CXLIX

**Verse 1. ***{Praise ye the Lord}* Heb. *Hallelujah*.

*{a new song}* Psal. 33.3. For his great and manifold benefits daily renewed on his Church, Lam. 3.23.

**V. 2. ***{him}* Heb. *them*, as Eccl. 12.1. Isa. 54.5.

*{that made him}* He gives two reasons why they should praise God with a new song. First, because he made them. Secondly, because he made them his people. In Hebrew it is, *In his makers:* to show the Trinity of persons, Isa. 54.5. Job 35.10.

**V. 3. ***{in the dance}* Or, *with the pipe*.

**V. 4. ***{For the Lord taketh pleasure in his people}* Psal. 35.27.

*{he will beautify the meek with salvation}* Not only free them from their enemies, but also make them glorious before the world.

**V. 5. ***{let them sing aloud upon their beds}* They should enjoy continual rest and liberty to praise God, and shall use their liberty both night and day, to that end, Psal. 63.6. Job 35.10. Eccl. 1.2.

**V. 6. ***{in their mouth}* Heb. *in their throat*; That is, they should sing aloud of God's famous acts.

*{a two-edged sword in their hand}* Either he means God would give them temporal victories, or the spiritual sword, Heb. 4.12. to rule men withal, Rev. 1.16.

**V. 7. ***{To execute vengeance upon the heathen}* This is chiefly accomplished in the Kingdom of Christ: when God's people, for just causes, execute God's judgments against his enemies: and it gives no liberty to any to revenge their private injuries.

**V. 8. ***{To bind their Kings with chains}* To what time this is to be referred, whether past, under the Law; or present, under the Gospel; or future, at the day of judgment, is a great question among Expositors. Our English Translators by their quotations in the margin; the Italian Notes, and divers others, understand it of the Canaanitish Kings, which were discomfited and put to death by God's people; by God's especial appointment, Deut. 7.1, &c. called therefore *the judgement written:* verse following. This is the most literal interpretation.

Yet others not less probable take the words of the conquest of Nations and Kingdoms by preaching, which though of itself contrary to all violence and effusion of blood, and therein contrary to Mahometanism, is nevertheless for the greater glory of it among men, set out unto us sometimes in military terms, as 2 Cor. 10.4, 5, 6. *For the weapons of our warfare,* &c. and elsewhere. Of the submission of Kings, see Isa. 49.22. and Psal. 2. and mention of *chains,* Isa. 45.14. *The labour of Egypt, and merchandise of Ethiopia,—they shall come after thee, in chains shall they come over,* &c. Which mention of chains, both here in the Psalmist, and in the Prophet, in the words quoted, tend only to this, the better to set out the magnificence of the victory, and ensuing triumph of the Church. So captive Kings were led in triumph among the Romans, *catenis onerati,* bound with divers chains: all which things, by all sober Christians are understood spiritually.

It is pure Mahometanism, to dream of any other sense, according to the letter; the spiritual sense, in such allegorical expressions, being the immediate and literal. But thirdly, there be, who refer all this to the day of judgment. Neither is it without some ground. They bring many places of the Scripture to that purpose. That which I take most notice of, is Enoch's prophecy, quoted by St. Jude; *Behold, the Lord cometh with ten thousand of his Saints, To execute judgement upon all,* &c. And this prophecy they would have to be here called by the Psalmist, *the judgement written.* And here also, besides the word *judgement,* though they observe it not, we have the Saints mentioned, *This honour have all his Saints,* verse 9, as in Enoch's prophecy, *with ten thousand of his Saints.* What others may think of it, I know not. I see nothing in this exposition, but is plausible enough. But the Reader shall have my good will, to make his choice.

*{the judgment written}* Hereby God binds the hands and minds of all his, to enterprise no further than he appoints in his written word. See Calvin here, with some others. A good item for those, who in imitation of the Turkish Dervises, or I know not whom, when they intend things, for which they have no warrant from any written Law either of God, or man; pretend inspirations.

*{This honour have all his Saints}* Psal. 148.14.

# PSALM CL

**Verse 1. ***{Praise ye the Lord}* Heb. *Hallelujah*.

*{in his sanctuary}* Or, *for his sanctuary*: That is, for revealing himself in his ordinances. So verse 2. *in his mighty acts:* That is, for them.

*{in the firmament of his power}* For his wonderful power appearing in the firmament, which in Hebrew is called, a *stretching out,* or, *spreading abroad.* And therein the mighty work of God shines.

**V. 2. ***{excellent greatness}* Or, *greatness of greatness, or, abundance of greatness*.

**V. 3. ***{with the sound of the trumpet}* He exhorts them, that they might praise God the better, to stir up their joy with musical instruments. And if musical instruments, as is granted, had that power then; how have they changed their nature since?

*{trumpet}* Or, *cornet*.

**V. 4. ***{dance}* Or, *pipe*.

**V. 5. ***{loud}* Heb. *of report*, Isa. 33.3.

*{high}* Heb. *shouting*.

**V. 6. ***{Let every thing that hath breath, praise the Lord}* That is, every man, Deut. 20.16. Josh. 11.11. So sometimes flesh is put for man. He shows, that all men have cause to praise God. Let us therefore, never cease praising him here, till we be taken from hence, into his heavenly Kingdom; which he has prepared for his, to sing of his praise for ever. Amen, and Amen.

*{thing}* Heb. *breath*.

*{Praise ye the Lord}* Eph. 5.19. Col. 3.16.
